ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u6	Thank you, Marshal. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! The 500+ family programme is a system support for Polish families, it is a program that will give the family such a powerful injection of cash for the first time since 1989. That's good, because social studies show that the main barrier to having children is the lack of stability in the material situation of the family. I hope it's the support that families get, that bad trend when it comes to natural growth, it's just gonna slow down. Each family with a minimum of two underage children will be able to receive 500 PLN for the second and the next child. For families with income below 800 PLN per person, each child will receive support, including an only child, and for families with children with disabilities the income criterion is higher and is 1200 PLN net. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u7	Thank you very much. The question will be answered by Undersecretary of State Bartosz Marczuk.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u8	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mrs. Member! When it comes to issues related to what you are talking about, i.e. with a broad plan or a broad support programme, it is indeed after 1989 the first such powerful support programme for families raising children, aimed at young Poles - we care very much about them. Indeed, research shows that as we ask Poles how many children they want to have, they say they want to have far more than they have, on average over two. We've got these kids... The implementation is at 1.3. This is one of the world's lowest fertility rates, and this gap between realized and expected births is one of the largest in the world. As we later ask Poles why this is happening, they - social diagnosis, research of the Warsaw Enterprise Institute, CBOS - are telling us clearly: We are afraid for our economic future. Therefore, this programme aims perfectly for issues related to how we respond to Poles' concerns: we simply reduce these concerns. As for the assumptions, it is indeed what you say: for the second child and another, without income threshold, all families, until the 18th year of the child's life, for the first child, if it is less than 800 PLN net per person in the family, and if it is a disabled child - 1200 PLN. What are the results of the consultation? What's gonna change? Indeed, we consulted the programme very widely, not only in the standard way in which public consultations usually take place, so we sent it to social partners, to many organisations dealing with family matters, but also, as you know perfectly well, we drove around all the voivodships - Minister Elżbieta Rafalska and all the vice-ministers - we consulted this programme in voivodships. We will take a lot of things into account from this consultation, with the fact that - and this is important information on the timetable, I am about to go to it - today the project is to be dealt with by a permanent committee of the Council of Ministers and there will be settlements. I can, for example. to say that we took note - the attention came from Łódź, very concrete but also very useful - and in the article. 8, which speaks of wasting money - the point is that the municipalities can not only buy things, that is to say, provide this assistance in kind, in a situation where they come to the conclusion that there is waste - we added the word "service," meaning, in other words, the municipality will also be able to pay tuition fees or buy colonies for such a child. And now the most important, that is the timetable and preparation of local governments. As far as the timetable is concerned, as I said, today the project is already being dealt with by the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers, we are after a consultation which ended on 22 January. At one of the forthcoming meetings of the Council of Ministers, the law will already deal with the government. Now prepare the local governments for payouts. That's probably the most important thing, because there's actually a lot of doubt here. That is, we believe, for at least seven reasons, that there will be no big problem, that from April 1st in local governments will start the selection of proposals. First reason: local governments already have an instrumentarium resulting from the Family Benefits Act to pay this kind of benefit, i.e. it is not a complete novelty for them, it is not necessary to place A new system, they have a computer system. Two, they get extra money for it. What the Prime Minister stressed at the joint government and local government committee meeting, that we do not give additional tasks to local governments without money. 2% , and it is included in the law, the amount that will be paid in the form of a benefit, ends up in local government accounts. Let me remind you, this year it is PLN 340 million, that is 2% from the amount of 17 billion, and next year PLN 460 million, that is 2% from the amount of PLN 23 billion, when the programme will be operational all year round. We also made a public commitment - reason number three - that we will carry out training as the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy in all voivodships. Our employees will be training local government officials to carry out this program efficiently. We're planning it for March. There will be our experts, both from the Department of Family Policy and the Department of Computer Science. Four, the bill provides that in the first period when people submit applications, they have three months to do so. If they submit these applications even in the third month, there will be an equalisation from 1 April. Hence, it can be expected that some people will not go straightaway to submit this proposal, and therefore there will be no dramatic upheaval. Five, after consulting local self-government voivodships, who were really many at these meetings, told us about about about a six-week vacatio legis when it comes to preparation. We provide in the Act, as you know, a minimum month, which may turn out to be approx. Fifty-six weeks. The position of the joint government committee is about 3 months. Hence the date of 1 July is a date in quotes, euphemistically speaking, and not even euphemistically, violated, because even if we predict, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that the bill will come into force or will be published at the end of February, then 3 months from that they give us March, April, May, and not July 1. It doesn't go out in any way on July 1st. And the last word, in the article. The 43th Act provides that after a week of publication of the Act, and not the entry into force of the Act, local governments will already receive money for preparation, so it will not be so that local governments will only get money for preparation from 1 April. From here we acknowledge, we hope... Of course, when we treat local governments as partners, we realize that we have to work very, very hard with them here. They will bear the burden of implementing this law. But we think the date of 1 April is the date when the selection of applications can start. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u20	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am so glad that this question has been asked. Indeed, as Minister Szyszko said at the Paris conference, geothermal is a great opportunity for Poland, a great opportunity in terms of the use of renewable energy sources. It is a pity that a country from the Civic Platform 8 years ago stopped the development of Toruń geothermals, because we would already have much more experience. What I'm trying to say right now is that the whole geothermal program is going to be re-created. It will be completely apolitical and will serve to use this warmth primarily for the people, for the inhabitants. It can therefore be said that some programmes will be set up for the comprehensive use of these geothermal sources, but above all for heating purposes. As you have said, they will be programmes for those who will use geothermal sources for heating purposes. It can't be that there's only going to be these sources. for aquaparks. Such parks will, of course, be able to be created if this heat is sufficient, but it is generally about heating targets. At the moment we are working on special programs not only related to search, but also to exploitation, because so far, as you know perfectly well, there was a programme that was subsidised in 50% or sometimes in 70%. only and only geothermal recognition. So these were test holes, but there was no provision for injection holes. It's kind of a closed system. It is based on the fact that geothermal water is drawn - they have, as in Konin, high parameters, I have heard that even above 100°C, the Member spoke of 95°C - then through heat exchangers this heat is returned to heating networks, and then, because this water cools down, it is crowded back into the aquifer. It's important to remember, too. That we can't dredge up aquifers. The aquifer layers are extremely important not only from the point of view of geothermals, but also from the point of view of the water resources of the country, because Poland, fortunately, has good deposits of groundwater, which are used primarily to supply the population with water. As far as certain details are concerned, I would like to say that yesterday there was a meeting at the National Fund for Environmental Protection and Water Management on the comprehensive use of geothermal sources. We would like to support, above all, local authorities, as I said earlier, in the comprehensive use of these sources. Here, which I stress once again, it is primarily intended to serve people. Poland has really very rich resources. With regard to operational programmes, in the axis, there is a sub-measure under Action 1.1.1 called: Support for investments in renewable energy generation, including the connection of these sources to the distribution and transmission network. This is where geothermal energy projects with a capacity above 2 MWt should be supported. However, the Ministry of Development has not yet decided on the entity implementing these sources. We would like it to be a national fund, because the national fund has resources from other sources, including so-called substitution fees, which in cogeneration with these EU funds could support these larger programmes. So that we anticipate very strong support. We would like to launch pilot programmes as soon as possible to show other local authorities what the possibilities of using geothermal energy sources are. Thank you very much.

The competence to reduce the population of animals in an emergency situation of life, health and human economy also gives the Sejm of the voivodship the Animal Protection Act. He may adopt an appropriate resolution in this regard. As the practice shows, the use of the above-mentioned instruments has proved insufficient so far, and we will therefore prepare a change of law in this area as regards competence to make the law more effective. In the opinion of experts, the level guaranteeing the reduction of the risk associated with the spread of ASF in the eastern regions of Poland, in particular from the territory of Ukraine, is to achieve the density of the population of feral pigs to a level of 0,5 individuals per km. Such measures should be taken in a belt about 50 km wide from the border of the restricted area in the Podlaskie Voivodeship and along the eastern border of the State. Meanwhile, the population of wild boars, which is a potential reservoir of this disease, in some areas, exceeds significantly the level of 1 person per km. Therefore, despite the fact that no outbreaks and cases of ASF have so far been found in the above-mentioned zone and the epizootic situation is stable, I decided to take forward measures which aim to ensure the best possible protection of Poland's territory from the entry of the disease across the eastern border, where the epizootic situation is not fully known. The actions taken by me are the ordering powers of the various bodies involved in reducing or reducing the population of boars, scattered in many legal acts. To this end, for inter-ministerial arrangements and public consultation, I have addressed a draft regulation on ordering wild boar's sanitary discharge, which envisages a reduction of the population to a scientifically safe level of 0.5 per km. Maintaining the density of the feral population at such a low level will reduce the transmission of African swine fever virus between feral packs and thus better protect against virus penetration into areas where the disease has not yet occurred. The measures provided for in this Regulation are in accordance with the opinion of the Standing Committee on Plants, Animals, Food and Feed, As part of the planned measures to achieve feral density of 0.5 per km, approximately 40,000 animals must be shot off in the above-mentioned belt. wild boar art. The estimated cost of this task is about PLN 12.6 million. Finally, I would like to stress that my action is aimed at reducing the population of healthy boars, because they are a potential reservoir of disease and an important factor for its spread. At the same time, I would like to stress that the spread of ASF outside the Podlaskie Voivodeship can have disastrous economic consequences not only for Poland but also for the whole European Union. For this reason, I believe that there is a need to act decisively when it comes to reducing wild boar populations. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u88	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! In 2013, for the first time, a comprehensive assessment of the scientific and research and development activities of scientific units was carried out in accordance with the principles laid down in the provisions issued under the Law of 30 April 2010 on the principles of funding for science. Scientific units have been evaluated by the Scientific Units Evaluation Committee in accordance with the criteria and specific parameters set out in the Regulation of the Minister for Science and Higher Education of 13 July 2012 on the criteria and modalities for the award of the scientific category to scientific entities. The assessment was based on the information provided by the scientific units in the survey of the scientific unit. The above assessment was strictly parametrised. To determine the scientific category for individual scientific units, an algorithm was used based on the method of comparing pairs of scientific units using a weighted relationship of transversal. A number of reservations were raised by the scientific community in relation to the principles adopted, including: concerning an incomprehensible and complex algorithm. The comments also dealt with problems related to the assessment of entities operating in different fields of science and insufficient consideration of utilitarian achievements. Critical comments and experience from the evaluation have led to the need to amend the provisions on evaluation of scientific units and the award of scientific categories. I therefore ask you to answer the following questions. Firstly, what major changes have been made in the evaluation of scientific units in relation to the evaluation carried out in 2013? Secondly, are the changes made in line with the comments and proposals made by the scientific community? Thirdly, whether the Ministry of Science and Higher Education plans to make changes in this area and what are further forward-looking plans of the Ministry, especially in the context of the basic dilemma, whether the assessment of scientific units should be parametric and largely quantified, [[♪ The bell ♪]] or should it evaluate towards an expert assessment?
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u89	Thank you very much, sir. And please take the floor of Minister Leszek Sirka. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u90	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Thank you very much for these very important questions about parametric evaluation of scientific units. Well, as you have rightly pointed out, these parametric assessments have not only aroused interest, they have also partially upset the scientific community with regard to the parametric assessment of 2013, because many reservations about this parameterization have not been taken into account in the parametric assessment. The main questions raised by the scientific community on the 2013 parametric evaluation were, inter alia,: simplifying the way, the algorithm of pair comparisons in terms of evaluation of scientific units, as well as modifying the way in which scientific bodies carry out scientific activities in different fields or disciplines of science are assessed. Here, before 2013, in the opinion of the scientific community, these individuals were not properly assessed. It was also suggested to increase the importance in the assessment of the scientific units of their innovative activities and to take into account in the evaluation of research projects carried out by the scientific unit the role of the units in the implementation of the projects. It is about coordination or the execution of a specific task. These changes and these proposals have already been addressed, at least in part, in the Regulation of the Minister for Science and Higher Education of 27 October 2015 on the criteria and modalities for the award of the scientific category to scientific bodies. Under this Regulation, the so-called "market economy operator' was granted and established. a group of joint assessments with regard to the so-called. non-university scientific units. This is the first thing that has already been done. Then the algorithm used to determine the category of scientific scientific units was simplified. In the previous categorization, the units were compared in pairs and in groups of joint assessments, it was completely opaque, it could not actually be completed later. Units could not be themselves in a specific position which resulted from the categorization. Now the units will be compared with the reference unit, which is a reasonable change. The role of innovative achievements has also been enhanced. Among other things, new evaluation parameters have been introduced concerning the revenues of the scientific entity for the sale of products and applications for scientific research results or development work will be included in the assessment of the entity. What is also important and what has been raised by scientific bodies is a change in how to assess the participation of individuals in projects involving research and development. According to the new proposal, activity will be scored in the coordination and implementation of these projects. It is well known that in various projects different entities play a different role - some simply carry out this project and participate in its implementation, some are coordinators of such projects. These are indeed different roles, which of course need to be appreciated and taken into account. Moreover, what is important, especially from the humanistic point of view of science, was introduced an increased score for monographs and artistic works. The so-called ones appeared. outstanding monographs, i.e. awarded with a prestigious award, for which 50 points can be awarded according to the current proposal and regulation, that is a very high assessment. In addition, the mobility of workers of the scientific unit has been taken into account, and therefore, as part of the parametric evaluation, the scientific stays of workers will also be assessed. The changes also concern the identification of category A+. In accordance with the Regulation, category A+ will be derived from category units And so it's going to be a subset of the categories. A, and will take into account six of the following criteria: the importance of achievements taken into account in criterion one, i.e. outstanding scientific or creative achievements, participation in the dissemination of scientific achievements, participation in the creation of strategic research infrastructure, the importance of research results or development work for the economic development of Poland, scientific and artistic achievements and participation in international research projects. Well, I would like to say that the Ministry believes that, although these changes are going in the right direction, they are not yet sufficient, because there is no emphasis on an important expert role in the evaluation of scientific institutions. Therefore, a new regulation will be introduced in 2016, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which will take into account the expert assessment and the number of achievements on the basis of which category A, B or C will be initially granted will be limited from 3N to 2N. The idea is simply that scientific institutions should create real science, real works, not publish causal works that are irrelevant, which are only done for future parameterization. As regards also the target changes that we will be implementing as early as 2021, there will be much more emphasis on expert assessment, including international experts, which seems to be a global standard and from which we cannot escape.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u93	(PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, That is a very important problem. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u117	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen of the Ministers! Dear Parliamentaries! The revolutionary judgment of the Court of Justice of the European Union of September 2015 results in many changes in the functioning of local governments. Municipalities will have to, among other things. take over the obligation to settle the VAT of its budgetary units and budgetary establishments, to submit one declaration to the municipality together with the organisational units, those without legal personality, to lean on VAT in tasks carried out with the participation of the European Union. There are problems ahead of local government units with regard to VAT clearance, where VAT, in accordance with the rules, may be ineligible expenditure, i.e. one which is not refundable from EU funds and the municipality had the possibility to settle it with the tax office. Therefore, the municipality should reimburse the relevant part of the subsidy to the Union budget. In the sense of local authorities, the draft VAT bill and the reimbursement of European funds leave the municipalities too little time, because only until 1 March 2016, to submit information on their own situation and to make a declaration that they do not settle VAT with the tax office and the European Union subsidy remains unchanged or that they settle VAT with the tax office and must return the subsidy together with interest. The issue of interest raises a lot of doubts, because the problem of reimbursement was not caused by local governments. Another point is the short time limit for the reimbursement of the Union grant in part of VAT by 1 March 2016. The government must first commit its own resources and wait for them to be returned from the tax office. The amendment of the rules may cause, inter alia, increasing the employment of workers and the need to adapt the financial and accounting systems to the new requirements. All of these will result in financial expenditure. Therefore, ladies and gentlemen, the honourable Minister, the local authorities are asking for a clear interpretation of the rules so as to avoid the costs and errors involved. Thank you very much. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u192	Thank you very much, sir. Please, Mr Paweł, the PSL club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u193	Mrs. Marshal! Gentlemen of Ministers! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party to express my opinion on the draft law amending the law on military service of professional soldiers. The provisions of the Act of 11 September 2003 on the military service of professional soldiers for the performance of contract service by a soldier state that they can be fulfilled for a maximum period of 12 years. The first major change concerns soldiers engaged in contract service and is to abolish a maximum of 12 years as a time limit for such service. According to the project, the soldier will be able to extend the service to the retirement age, which is like any other professional soldier. In this way, after obtaining an adequate age, he will be entitled to a pension, housing and other benefits. This mainly applies to private soldiers, because they are most often in contract service. The second part of the project concerns a revision of the provision setting out the rules for the appointment of soldiers to senior posts. The current rules provide that a soldier appointed to a higher post shall also be appointed to a higher military degree, which shall be assigned to the post concerned. The amendment assumes that, in specific cases, the Minister of National Defence will be able to appoint a soldier as a military position of two or more military degrees higher than the rank held by the soldier concerned without having to appoint him to a higher degree. Greater responsibilities would be offset by a special supplement that will cover the differences between your rank and your position. For example, a major soldier will be able to be appointed as a full colonel and will not be promoted, will remain as a major. The compensation for greater responsibilities will be the compensatory allowance introduced by this project. Thus, a soldier in the position of a colonel will be a major, but during his duties as a colonel will receive compensation equal to the difference between these grades. The acquisition of military degrees is conditional on gaining further levels of education, skills and further exercises. It was a matter of a unit being commanded by an officer with specific powers. This is similar in the case of taking positions in headquarters and headquarters. The revision opens the gate in this way. There is, however, a guarantee in the Act that there can be no situation in which soldiers are higher than superiors in the unit, but this can be easily obtained, e.g. by releasing the entire command of a given unit or organizational structure. We are surprised that such an important bill amending or introducing changes in the Polish army is a parliamentary project, not a government one. A parliamentary project means no government position on the subject, no resort arrangements, no detailed calculations of the burden on the state budget. In the explanatory memorandum we can read that the project does not have financial implications for the state budget and local government units in 3 years, and in 10 years it is estimated that the costs of entering into force for the budget of the proposed law will be around PLN 1.5 billion. For us, these are calculations taken absolutely from the ceiling. You can't tell exactly what the burden will be because you don't know how many percent of the soldiers will retire after 15 years. The Polish People's Party, your marshal, makes amendments to the parliamentary bill amending the law on military service of professional soldiers. These amendments, this amendment proposal, I'm putting in your hands the marshal. The Polish People's Party agrees with point one, and as for point two, [[♪ The bell ♪]] where once again the fullness of power is given to one person, in this case the Minister of National Defence, is full of doubts. Hence our amendment. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u231	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I have the honour to present the report of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs on the Government's draft law on the re-use of public sector information, print No 141. However, I would like to make it quite clear to the High House that this important piece of legislation is being discussed today at today's Parliament meeting. The Committee on Administration and Home Affairs, following the decision of the Marshal of the Sejm, considered this government draft law on the re-use of public sector information and consulted on this matter the opinion of the Committee on Culture and Media, which was positive for this project. She subsequently appointed an extraordinary subcommittee to consider this draft law, which examined the draft at two meetings - on 12 and 14 January this year. In addition to members, representatives of the Ministry of Digital Affairs and the State Secretary Witold Kołodziejski present here, as well as representatives of the Ministry of Culture and National Heritage, the General Data Protection Officer and NGOs participated in the work of the committee and subcommittee. The work focused on the issue of access to and re-use of public information. The opinion of the Sejm Analysis Office was also received on the project, and very carefully analysed during the legislative work. On 26 January, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs examined the report of the subcommittee. These works - as I stress - were very detailed and substantive. The report of the Sub-Committee on Administration and Home Affairs introduced nine changes, both substantive, editorial and legislative. I would also like to inform the High House that the Foreign Minister issued today an opinion on the compatibility of the draft law with European Union law, in which he stated that the provisions of this draft law on the re-use of public sector information are in line with European law. What are the key solutions provided for in the draft law on the re-use of public sector information? Firstly, the new law on the re-use of public sector information implements the European Directive 2013/37/EU of the European Parliament and of the Council of 26 June 2013 amending Directive 2003/98/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 November 2003 on the re-use of public sector information. Secondly, the Act will lay down rules and procedures for the provision and transmission of public sector information for re-use, entities which make such information available or provide it, conditions for re-use and rules for the fixing of re-use charges. Thirdly, the law will extend the possibility of re-use of archives, museums and libraries to which copyright has expired. It's so-called. Public domain. Until now, the re-use rules have been governed by Chapter 2a of the Act of 6 September 2001 on access to public information and concerned only public information. Fourthly, the Act contains exemptions and restrictions on the application of the provisions of the Act corresponding to the European Directive I mentioned just now, e.g. because of the privacy of a natural person or when intellectual property rights are conferred on third parties. Fifthly, the Act introduces a definition of public sector information that will be re-used and a definition of re-use. Because of the so-called. Re-use should be understood the use by natural persons, legal persons and organisational entities without legal personality of public sector information for commercial or non-commercial purposes other than the original public purpose for which the information was produced. By contrast, public sector information should mean any content, regardless of how it is maintained - in paper, electronic, sound, visual or audiovisual form - held by public sector entities which are enumerated in this project. Sixth, due to the specific nature of the resources held by archives, museums and libraries, these institutions will be able to determine the conditions for re-use and charging to a wider extent than other entities, but in accordance with the provisions set out in the introduction of European Directive 2013/37/EU by me. It is worth noting that the new law empowers the Minister of Culture and National Heritage to issue a regulation setting maximum rates of charges for commercial re-use that can be determined by e.g. museums. Seventhly, the Act will set out the procedure for making public sector information available on request and on the elements of the proposal and will introduce the possibility of re-use in a permanent and direct manner, in real time, of public sector information collected and stored in the electronic system of the obliged entity. After the eighth, in order to increase access to public resources, the provisions of the Act of 6 September 2001 on access to public information on the central repository of public information and the provisions of the Act of 14 July 1983 on national archives and archives will also be revised. Next: What were the key changes made to the bill as a result of committee and subcommittee meetings? Firstly, it was an extension of the scope of the subjective draft law on the Institute of Meteorology and Water Management and the State Institute of Geology, as well as the introduction in the Act of 18 July 2001. Water law appropriate changes, which allow free provision of meteorological data for reuse. These provisions, which I would like to stress, will enter into force on 1 January 2017. I would like to thank here for the very creative work of NGOs, which have raised the issue of sharing these data for 20 years, and above all for the very effective cooperation between the Ministry of Digitalisation and the Ministry of the Environment. This is a very important change, which also allows a number of actions relevant to the practical functioning of citizens, NGOs and, above all, entrepreneurs. Secondly, the application of the provisions of the Act to public sector information transmitted between public sector entities for the purpose of carrying out legal tasks has been excluded. Thirdly, the committee clarified the concept of public sector information. Fourthly, the committee has determined the legal form in which the conditions or charges for the re-use of public sector information made available without a request are to be established by the obliged entities. Fifthly, the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs clarified the scope of the mandate for the Minister responsible for IT to issue a Regulation on information resources to be made available in the central repository of public information. And sixthly, during consultations between the Legislative Office and the Government Administration, in particular the Ministry of Digitalisation, the non-constitutional provisions of the Law of 16 February 2007 on oil stocks, petroleum products and natural gas and the rules for dealing with situations of fuel security and disturbances on the oil market, which provide that the individual data contained in the register, with the exception of the name of the manufacturer and the trader, are not public information and are made available under the Act of 6 September 2001 on access to public information. As amended, these data constitute a legally protected secret and may be made available to certain entities for the purposes set out in those provisions. So much for me to provide as much information as possible on the work preceding today's second reading of the Act. On behalf of the ladies and gentlemen of the House, members of the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs and the subcommittees with which we all worked together, I would like to thank you for a very substantive and thorough analysis of the various provisions of this law, from both the Ministry of Digital Affairs and the Ministry of the Environment. Here I have presented some of the key changes that have been made in the course of the work. In particular, I would also like to thank for the very active cooperation of non-governmental organisations, which, in many ways and very much to say, effectively and, above all, cooperate with the Committee on Administration and Home Affairs during the process of this project. So that I can finally say with satisfaction that this draft law, which I hope will soon be passed by Parliament, will be a good offer for both citizens and entrepreneurs in the area, for example. innovation, creating new solutions that will be citizen-friendly. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

In view of the extended scope of re-use, it has become necessary to separate the provisions on re-use from the Act of 6 September 2001 on access to public information contained in Chapter 2a thereof and to regulate the rules and modalities for re-use of public sector information in the proposed law. In principle, documents cannot be considered to be within the meaning of Article. 2 Directive 2003/98 of the European Parliament is a document of the content or part of the content irrespective of how it is maintained (in paper, electronic, audible, visual or audiovisual form) held by the obliged entity, which constitutes public information within the meaning of the Act on access to public information. The main change in the proposed changes is to extend the scope of the public information, the selected resources of libraries and museums and archives to be re-used. There is also a simultaneous extension of the scope of this catalogue of documents made available for reuse, i.e. those held by libraries, archives and public museums. All documents made available by public sector bodies can therefore be re-used for any purpose, whether commercial or non-commercial, unless they are protected by third party intellectual property rights, which ensures the maintenance of copyright and copyright protection. The Act is not subject to resources held, among others. public broadcasting units, public and local cultural institutions, with the exception of museums, libraries and archives referred to above, but also the resources of universities, scientific units and kindergartens, schools, educational centres, etc., unless this information was already publicly available in the Public Information Bulletin. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u253	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the pleasure and the honour to present to you the government information about Polish policy towards the European Union, in the European Union during the Luxembourg Presidency. The Presidency is only 6 months old, most of the important processes, complex decision-making processes, political and legislative processes in the European Union take much longer, of course, but I think it is a very good time to stop for a moment and look at the whole of European affairs, the whole of Polish policy in the European Union, also to restore a certain proportion of our perception of our relations in the European Union, which is sometimes disturbed by more or less spectacular events. I think this debate and this information are a very good opportunity to notice. It is also a very good opportunity to note that the Presidency's programme, prepared by a very experienced administration, by a very experienced government of a country which has a very long membership time in the European Union, has given a good political balance, a balance of the various objectives that the European Union has set before itself, which it offers to the Member States, but, while being implemented and well planned, it has clearly been violated by external events that no one could plan. I am thinking, above all, of two issues which have fallen on the shoulders of the Luxembourg Presidency, which have not yet been fully resolved and which have largely dominated the energy of the entire decision-making system of EU politicians, ministers in particular sectors, including Heads of State or Government. I am referring, above all, to the loss of control of the migration process within the European Union and at the borders of the European Union, and, on the other hand, to tragic events in Paris, which have brought us back to a sense of deep concentration, a strong focus on internal security issues. These are issues that are also worth staying on because, at least in this first area, during the Luxembourg Presidency, decisions have been taken which have caused great political tensions, including in Poland, and which have still not given us a clear indication of how to deal with this most serious, most crucial problem, in my view, the existential problem facing the European Union and what is linked to the loss of control of migration processes to the Union and within the Union. The first wave of these decisions, which was a direct response to the collapse of the capacity of countries to manage the migration process, even those countries that have a very long tradition and huge experience in migration processes, was the work of creating a mechanism that would be bureaucratic and quite centralised in managing migrant groups within the European Union. It was decided, in my opinion, unhappy to use the procedure, to take decisions by qualified majority, which opened the way for the isolation of the interests and opinions of some Member States of the European Union, which created a sense of the huge political cost of this decision, which resulted in the introduction of the so-called. a relocation mechanism, which, despite the enormous political costs that continue today, has not brought any of the expected results, and the effects have been very modest since the beginning. The scale of this process has never responded to the real migratory pressure we have encountered in the European Union. Despite this relatively modest scale and the mechanism that he promised to take, he announced a solution to the uneven migratory pressure on the selected Member States of the European Union, the problem was not solved, of course, as expected by us. Today, this decision is not only implemented by countries that have raised doubts about such a procedure and such a mechanism for decision-making and policy-making, but also by all countries, including those who have had great hopes of implementing such a policy based on this mechanism from the outset. Luckily, with the next weeks, the European Union's agenda in this area was moving towards solutions that are much closer to Polish policy in this area, Polish preferences, Polish beliefs in this area. I am thinking of strengthening political discourse, often also legislative decisions in the area of identification of applicants for international protection. I am referring to strengthening attention around the security issues of this process. Finally, I am thinking of a clearly strengthened focus on readmission processes and, most importantly, the sealing of the European Union's external borders and the strengthening of cooperation with third countries, in particular those that are the source of the migratory wave, often the refugee wave, which in turn indirectly influences the feeling of losing control of many political processes in the Union, finally creating significant political instability in key EU countries. This is a positive evolution, but we must remember that none of these roads, none of these concepts has been closed and that Poland is in intensive, very intensive political dialogue today in order to shape this final policy in this area. On the one hand, the policy of restoring control over the migration wave, on the other hand, restoring citizens' confidence in their own countries in the right way. The second individual issue, which had an important impact on political, legislative issues in the European Union during the Luxembourg Presidency, is, of course, tragic events in Paris. Unfortunately, it was only the terrorist attack that caused us to restore the proper course of cases with regard to the Passenger Data Directive. This directive has been waiting for adoption for many years as a result of a political misunderstanding between the EU institutions, as well as between certain countries. It was adopted only after these tragic events and significantly equips the competent institutions of the Member States with this information which can help to combat terrorist activities in fact, strengthened. The second aspect of this issue is, of course, cooperation with third countries, including in the area of counter-terrorism. The European Union has decided, to a large extent, through Mrs Federici Mogherini and her responsibility in the area of external policy, the European Union has decided to cooperate with the countries of the Middle East region, North Africa, including Turkey in the field of technical assistance, but also with financial assistance that would help these countries, of course, in particular those who wish it, because not all countries are equally interested in doing so, in an important way to improve the state of home affairs and control in matters of justice, home affairs and internal security. This is an important direction, which, of course, we are counting, will continue in the coming years. It was also undertaken to restore the reinforced attention around the Arms Directive. This directive is being worked on today, and work on it continues. She raises reasonable doubts in certain areas. I am convinced that a good compromise, which will balance the different points on this issue in general with regard to the issue of the availability of weapons, is achievable so that, on the one hand, control of arms trafficking has a real dimension in the European Union, but on the other hand, it does not take place at the expense of those who have a legitimate right to own and use weapons, such as. territorial defence in some Member States or reconstruction groups in some Member States. Let us return to the regular elements, to the regular work plan of the Luxembourg Presidency, as it was created, announced and implemented last June. I think it's worth mentioning a few initiatives. Most of them will certainly be taken creatively by the next Dutch Presidency. I will mention those that are important and crucial also from a Polish point of view and open up some possibility of political debate as well. Firstly, another report on the state of the internal market has appeared. Poland has always attached great importance to this aspect of membership since the beginning of its membership, seeing in the internal market the greatest chance for not only material, levelled competition on a large European market, but also to speed up modernisation processes, speeding up the processes of increasing the competitiveness of the Polish economy. We welcomed Luxembourg's interest in this matter, and we welcomed the fact that the Presidency, when offering a whole group of provisions called a service passport, a certain concept of a service passport, highlighted the important role of small and medium-sized enterprises in the Community market. The Community market in this area, particularly in the area of services, is still unfinished and all efforts made by all participants in this process to ensure that this market is completed as far as possible will always be supported by Poland. An important new aspect of the internal market is, of course, the digital market. This is a huge group of legislative projects. They address a very wide range of issues that should be regulated, for example because the market is developing very rapidly and a growing group of European consumers are willing to refer to this aspect of the market as a place to meet their expectations. There is a legitimate need for Europe with its 500 million consumers to be able to shape the standards that meet our requirements, our belief in the proper relationship between the parties to the digital market. The third thing that is definitely worth remembering is the mobility package. This is a package that is very well named. This title makes a very positive impression, while the content brings a certain amount of controversy. The Mobility Package, which is to refer to the revision of the Regulation on the coordination of social security systems, is, on the other hand, to a targeted revision of the Posting of Workers Directive, if it were to be adopted in the form proposed, would bring us disproportionate measures from the point of view of the objectives which the package wants to achieve, objectives which are essentially legitimate: better protection of social rights, better position for those who provide work or services on the Community market. However, these disproportionate measures may mean that, as a result, we will receive a market which will be burdened with protectionism instruments, instruments which, instead of developing the Community market, limit it at the expense of Central Europe, of course, in the first place, but, in fact, at the expense of the whole Community market. There is further progress and political dialogue on this issue, but also legislative action. Of course, energy security was an important aspect of these six months. This is a topic that has been very crucial for Poland for many years. We look in the EU institutions and in EU law for a significant strengthening of the energy security architecture in the European Union. During this Presidency, a summer package has emerged, which mostly refers to the new development of the Community electricity market. As for the direction, this is the right assumption, but given the very diverse state of the energy mix, the diverse state of energy policies of individual countries, often countries in our immediate neighbourhood, the diverse system of financing this energy change, it is necessary to avoid a situation in which the common electricity market will result in Poland becoming a country importing electricity. This is an important dimension, it draws the attention of the government and complex legislative work in this area will certainly focus on this particular aspect of shaping the electricity market so that it is also beneficial for electricity consumers in Central Europe or Poland. An important aspect of energy security policy was the issue of the applicability or activation of the European Commission, as well as other EU institutions in the field of infrastructure investment. As a rule, Poland has always stressed that all infrastructure investments, regardless of how they are financed, how they are organised from the investment side, should equally be subject to the requirements of European energy market law. This involves, above all, the Nord Stream 2 investment planned today. We believe that the European Commission should be more active in this matter. During the last six months of the Luxembourg Presidency, there has been a lot going on in terms of the Polish government's contact with the outgoing and new government to make the European Commission aware of this problem, because there is of course a very serious risk that the European Commission will make suggestions to act passively on this issue. The Polish request to activate the European Commission on the application of EU law to this investment was clearly underlined in intensive contact with the European Commission, but was also underlined in the conclusions of the European Council held in December. We have also had important events in the area of climate policy, which also have an important impact on our internal climate policy discourse. Firstly, a European mandate for a global Paris climate conference was adopted. This mandate was adopted at EU level because at global level the Union is a regional partner of this agreement in many areas. This mandate, as expected by Poland, did not provide for the possibility of increasing the local, regional reduction target or the EU reduction target above those values which were agreed at a special European Council summit which referred to the new climate policy by 2030. Finally, the Luxembourg Presidency was responsible for the way in which the Polish - EU - and thus the Polish - representation was carried out in the framework of the Paris climate conference. It ended with proposals that are satisfactory from a Polish point of view mainly because they take into account the possibility of very diverse participation in this agreement, in line with the preferences of individual participants in the global agreement and the capacities of individual participants in the global agreement. If we applied similar logic within the European Union itself, we would certainly feel much more comfortable in terms of climate policy, which raises legitimate concerns. We have also started work on this internal issue under the Luxembourg Presidency on the legal implementation of the conclusions of the European Council on climate policy. Legislative work has begun on the revision of the European Emissions Trading Scheme Directive, the ETS. These are works which I believe are the arena of problem number one from a Polish point of view in the field of economic regulatory relations between Warsaw and Brussels. This will certainly come back, but it has started under the Luxembourg Presidency. It is worth noting at least two more cases. Firstly, the Eastern Partnership Summit in Riga. He, of course, routinely and in the right direction highlighted our expectation, common, European, but especially important in Poland, that free trade agreements and the process of freeing visa-free movement towards countries that are prepared and willing to do so, i.e. Georgia and Ukraine, should be accelerated to ensure that these entries are implemented. On the other hand, one type of profession can be seen in the political level of the conclusions of this summit. This summit was extremely passive in referring to the most important fact in our Eastern Partnership or in our eastern neighbourhood, Russia's aggression towards Ukraine, with enormous consequences for the condition of the eastern aspect of the neighbourhood policy, with which we have so far-reaching hopes. I hope that the next summit on this issue will allow us to better understand and evaluate what is happening on the Russian-Ukrainian border. The last issue, certainly not least, is the issue of a new formulation of the UK membership rules in the European Union. During the Luxembourg Presidency, this process has already started at a much more institutional level. The responsibility for carrying out this process in a smooth manner remains largely with the European Council today. On the other hand, the Presidency and subsequent presidencies will certainly play an important role in putting the political compromise in practice. Most of the requests, as you know, submitted by the United Kingdom, are perceived in Poland as not only postulates that can be accepted but simply useful. I am referring to the development, better guarantees for relations between the euro area and countries that have not decided to join the euro area. Poland is in such a position today, it is de facto a country outside the euro area. We want, as is the case with the United Kingdom, to ensure that all necessary and necessary anti-crisis measures in the euro area do not affect the integrity of the Community market, so that they are transparent and open to countries that, for various reasons, do not participate in the longer or shorter term in Economic and Monetary Union. It is certainly very right that the British intuition is to put the process of European integration much more firmly into the reality of true European democracy, and its place is national parliaments such as the Polish Sejm. This is the only way that can effectively lead to a European integration process, a European process in proper democratic legitimacy. This is, of course, a very long topic. For many, the European Parliament has been a sufficient institution to ensure democratic legitimacy for a very long time. Today we know and see clearly that if we really want to embed this process in the awareness of the citizens of the Member States of the European Union, if we really want to put it under democratic control, then this control can only be done effectively through national parliaments, with a much greater, more important role for national parliaments. In terms of economic governance, too, the UK calls for the European Union to limit its normal, legislative activities to pay significant attention to the costs of its regulations, which are quite welcome. This point of view coming directly from the experience of the British deregulation experience is as worthy of support as possible and the European Union can only bring clear profits. This is not a problem for the European Union, it is a solution for the European Union, but a constant problem which is an important negotiating problem in this whole four-cart set of issues that the United Kingdom expects is the issue of shaping, placing migrant workers in the internal market, particularly in the UK market. The UK has decided to open the labour market very quickly and to open it really, unlike many other countries in the European Union. Presumably this was one of the reasons why so many migrant workers decided to look for jobs and new opportunities there, because many other national markets, despite the accession of all the new countries in Central Europe, were not open. Poland, on the other hand, will continue to support the full respect of the principle of non-discrimination in every reform, with every compromise in this area. We understand the UK's needs in this matter, and in particular these are the needs of some type of process perception, because in statistics, the belief that migrant workers are some excessive burden on social systems is not confirmed. Poles, Hungarians, Lithuanians who migrate to the UK are professionally activated above the British average, are taxpayers and workers who create the welfare of this country. However, the perception of this process, a certain level of dissatisfaction with freedom of movement at all, and from this aspect of the common market, makes there a political need on the British side to make changes in this matter. Any changes that will be proposed to us in this process must comply with this one principle, the principle of non-discrimination, for example, for this reason, not only very selfish and narrow, but for example because we should not make compromises on the common market that will restrict it. In a way, we have always spoken the same voice with the United Kingdom, pointing out that if something has a clear, unambiguous and huge positive effect on European citizens, European businesses, it is the common market and the growing freedom to provide work, services, capital transfers in the common market. This issue is certainly at the heart of the negotiations on this issue today. This is the panorama of the issues that I believe were at least the most crucial during the six months of the well-prepared Luxembourg Presidency, the Presidency, which, I think, can be said, has managed to respond to the challenges that have suddenly fallen. Not all of these responses were satisfactory, they were certainly not complete, but that is why we expect that the continuation of these processes, particularly in terms of migration, will bring us much more complete solutions during the Dutch Presidency. ParlaMint-PL_2016-01-28-sejm-09-2.u255	Dear Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am very pleased on behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, for the first time as chairman of this committee, to present a report and present a draft resolution of the Sejm on the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union in July-December 2015 under the Luxembourg Presidency of the Council of the European Union. Information from the Council of Ministers on Poland's activities during the Luxembourg Presidency was adopted by the European Affairs Committee on 11 January this year. and on the same day handed over to the High House. The rapid transmission of information by the government to the Sejm allows us in Parliament to discuss the events of the Presidency almost immediately after its completion. The government's information, as in previous years, was prepared in cooperation with all ministers and central offices, and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is responsible for its presentation. This is already the 24th half-yearly report of the Council of Ministers for the Sejm and the Senate from the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union during the successive periods of the presidency. The European Union Affairs Commission considered the information provided in print No 181 at its 19th meeting on 27 January 2016. At this point, I would also like to thank the Marshal for agreeing to include this item on the agenda of this sitting. This allows us to present the information discussed at the committee meeting of yesterday to the High House on an ongoing basis. The information on behalf of the Council of Ministers was presented by Mr Konrad Szymański, State Secretary at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The current information is presented at a specific time, because two governments are responsible for its implementation. In November, there was a change of government, and Minister Konrad Szymański presented us with the actions of both the current government and the previous term of office. The Minister presented the progress and achievements of the Luxembourg Presidency through its priorities and key issues from Poland's point of view. The Luxembourg Presidency focused on the following issues: stimulating investment to boost economic growth, deepening the social dimension of the European Union, managing migration, but also the Minister drew attention to the issues of the Digital Single Market, the Mobility Package or the new UK position in the European Union. The Presidency's actions are also affected by current developments which are difficult to predict at the priority setting stage. As we all know, the Luxembourg Presidency has been dominated by the issue of mass migration to the countries of the European Union. Migrations proved to be a very serious problem, the Union faced a huge challenge, and the European Union's Committee on European Affairs at its meeting on 11 September 2015 jointly with the Committee on Home Affairs held a discussion on preparing Poland for the growing crisis related to the wave of refugees in Europe. Another challenge faced by the Luxembourg Presidency was terrorist attacks in Europe. As part of the fight against terrorism, the Luxembourg Presidency managed to reach political agreement within the Council of the European Union on the content of the draft directive on the use of Passenger Name Record data to prevent, detect, investigate and prosecute terrorist offences and serious crime. The Commission for European Union Affairs adopted the information on 25 November 2015. In the area of the functioning of the internal market of the European Union, Poland has actively supported the process of creating a digital single market. The Commission for European Union Affairs of the previous mandate on 23 July 2015 discussed the Communication from the European Commission on a Digital Single Market Strategy for Europe. The protection of the freedom of movement of persons and the provision of services remained a priority of Poland. Poland continued to oppose the individual measures taken by Germany and France to include foreign transport companies in the rules on posted workers and, consequently, to cover them with minimum conditions of employment. As President of the European Union Committee on Affairs, I would like to convey to the High House that the Luxembourg Presidency's time is another time of active work for the European Union's Committee on Affairs. During the Luxembourg presidency of the Council of the European Union, the committee held 27 meetings. Compared to the previous six-month presidential periods, the number of meetings held by our committee was slightly lower, but this is mainly due to the fact that the parliamentary elections were interrupted for more than a month. The Commission examined 292 European Union documents, adopted 7 opinions, including 4 opinions on EU documents. In the case of one of the documents, the committee found that the draft legislative act was not compatible with the principle of subsidiarity and the opinion of the committee was sent to the Presidents of the European Commission, the European Parliament and the Council in the framework of a political dialogue. The Commission has also given an opinion on a number of candidates for posts in the institutions of the European Union, including Janusz Wojciechowski's appointment as a member of the European Court of Auditors during this term of office. The Commission held discussions during its meetings, inter alia. on the results of the Eurozone Summit held on 12 July 2015, reports, so-called: a report by the five presidents on completing the European Economic and Monetary Union, the results of the 21st Conference of the Parties to the United Nations Framework Convention on Climate Change held in Paris, the state of use of European funds by Poland. In the framework of the committee, there was an extraordinary subcommittee on trade agreements between the European Union and third countries. On 8 October 2015, the Commission adopted a report on the work of this subcommittee. I might add that during the Luxembourg Presidency, the Sejm adopted 40 laws implementing European Union law. Act of 8 October 2010 on cooperation of the Council of Ministers with the Sejm and the Senate, i.e. the so-called. The co-operation law allowed for further information on the government's activities in the European arena, of which the European Union's Committee on Affairs both during the previous term and during this term of office has made reasonable and conscientious use of it. In view of the well-prepared written information provided by the Ministry of Foreign Affairs and thanking the Minister for his in-kind dialogue with the members of the committee, the European Union Commission decided to recommend to the High House the adoption of a resolution of the Sejm in which the Sejm of the Republic of Poland accepts information from the Council of Ministers about the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union between July and December 2015, chaired by Luxembourg in the Council of the European Union. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
